The non-fast routine at 0xd9d7d60 differs only in the second address (entry 754, 0xda01e68).

### O32 vpro-patch-4103 routines read in full

Each entry is one routine in the o32 IP30 library of the 4103 patch (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so`), read in full from its own disassembly. Sizes are the routine's own extent (the function table's size for several of these runs on into the static routines behind them). Offsets are this build's own: the context record is laid out differently from the 6.5.30 builds, and every FIFO store goes to the absolute window address 0x4000. A "settle prefix" below is the four-word sequence `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, sent first when the settle-needed word at context +0x9750 is set (which clears it).

- `DrawBitmapUsingPolyStipple` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda3f914, 3100 bytes):
  **Established.** Draws one bitmap through `LoadAndSendStippledQuads` (0xda3ef20, reached through the local slot at gp-32740 plus -4320) after switching the board to polygon-stipple mode, then puts the state back (arguments: a0 context, a1 bitmap record, a2 pointer to the bitmap bytes; the last two are saved at sp+516 and sp+520). It returns at once, with no value set, when the byte at context +0x16658 is nonzero. Otherwise: (1) it compares the pair at +0x7b40 and +0x7b44 with the pair at +0x16650 and +0x16654; when either differs it copies both across, sends block `0x1081d` with the two words (after a settle prefix when needed), and, when bit 20 of the word at +0x7a90 is clear, copies a four-word template from the data slot at gp-32616 plus 4144 to the stack, rewrites its four words with the 12-bit fields of the two pairs (low 12 bits and bits 23:12 of each word) and calls `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0, slot gp-30572) with request 0x6f64000d. It then sets bit 13 (0x2000) of the dirty word at +0x168d4, stores 2 in process word 0x1018 and rewrites the word at +0x2d44 with itself. When the pairs match this step is skipped, branch-likely. (2) It saves the word at +0x7b18 (sp+500), the 32 stipple words at +0x7a98 to +0x7b14 (16 words at +0x7a98 to +0x7ad4 to sp+428 to sp+488, the other 16 at +0x7ad8 to +0x7b14 to sp+364 to sp+424, one pair per pass), +0x7b1c (sp+492) and the word at +0x9748 (sp+360). When the byte at +0xa65c is set it calls `__glOdysseyUpdateRasterPos` (0xda68694, slot gp-31672) with the context, and it always calls `__glOdysseyImageCalcOdyEyeSpaceRasterPosZ` (0xdaa6688, slot gp-31188) and keeps the float it returns at sp+356. (3) When the pointer at +0x164c0 (the bound texture) is nonzero it takes the current texture coordinates at +0x120, +0x124 and +0x128 and divides all three by the word at +0x12c (computed as 1.0 divided by it in double precision) unless that word is zero. When the third is nonzero (compared as a double) and the word at +12 of the bound texture is 0x806f, it goes through the dispatch table at +0x17ec: it saves the error code returned by slot +492 (sp+112), the matrix mode (slot +500 with 0xba0, sp+108), pushes the projection, model-view and texture matrices (modes 0x1701, 0x1700, 0x1702 through slot +620, push through slot +640), and each time slot +492 returns 0x503 it instead reads the matrix with slot +496 (enumerants 0xba7, 0xba6 and 0xba8 into the buffers at sp+276, sp+212 and sp+148; a byte flag at sp+343, 342 or 341 records which). It loads identity (slot +608) in each, makes the projection an orthographic box through slot +632 (left 0.0, right the integer at +0xe6c, bottom 0.0, top the integer at +0xe70, near -1.0, far 1.0), applies slot +664 with 0.0, 0.0 and the word at +0x108, and saves the current colour (enumerant 0xb00 into the buffer at sp+132) and texture coordinate (0xb03 into the buffer at sp+116) with slot +496 before setting them from the words at +0xf4 (slot +1428, with that word as the argument) and the address +0x120 (slot +1660). Which GL call each slot is was inferred from its arguments. (4) It clears bits 4, 3 and 2 of +0x7b18 and sets bit 2, then clears bits 1:0, so bits 4:0 hold 4 (three stores of the word); it clears bit 11 of +0x7b1c when bit 6 of the word at +0x700 is set and bit 7 when bit 24 of +0x700 is set; it sets bit 4 of +0x7b1c and sends block `0x10404` with it. This build sends no `0x1043f` here; the callee sends it. When the polygon-mode words at +0x1e4 or +0x1e8 differ from GL_FILL (0x1b02) it saves +0x7b24 (sp+496), sets its bits 3:2 to 10 and bits 1:0 to 10, and sends `0x10415` with it. It clears the low 12 bits of the word at +0x9748 and sends block `0x10456` with the word. This build has no clip-plane block `0x10405`. Each send has its own settle prefix. (5) When the word at +0x168d0 is nonzero it calls `__glOdysseyValidateHardware` (0xda47694, slot gp-32192). It then calls `LoadAndSendStippledQuads` with the context, the bitmap record, the float from sp+356, the float from sp+352, the floats from sp+348 and sp+344 and the bytes pointer on the stack. (6) It restores: +0x7b18 from sp+500, the 32 stipple words (four words of each half per pass), and resends `0x1043f` with +0x7b18, `0x13c4e` with the 16 words at +0x7a98 to +0x7ad4, `0x13c4f` with the 16 words at +0x7ad8 to +0x7b14, `0x10404` with +0x7b1c restored from sp+492, `0x10415` with +0x7b24 restored from sp+496 when a polygon-mode word is not GL_FILL, and `0x10456` with +0x9748 restored from sp+360. When the bound texture is 3D and the third coordinate was nonzero it pops the three matrix modes (slot +636, or slot +612 on the saved matrix when the push had overflowed), restores the colour and texture coordinate, the matrix mode from sp+108, and finally calls `__glSetError` (0xdb1ac34, slot gp-26932) with the error code saved at sp+112. It returns with no value set on every path. Compared with the 6.5.30 N32 routine of the same name it has the same stages, uses the context offsets given here, writes the FIFO at absolute 0x4000, and sends no clip-plane block `0x10405`.
- `OpenPipeMemReference` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaaccc4, 516 bytes):
  **Established.** Marks a range as in use and makes the board hold it (arguments: a0 context, a1 group record, a2 range record, a3 new extent, and the mode as the fifth argument at 88(sp)). When the mode is 0, the word at range +0x1c is 1 and the word at range +0x18 plus the extent is above zero, it first drains the pipe by sending 17 constant words to the FIFO at 0x4000: `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, `0x10019`, `0x10443`, 150, four `0x10046`, then, after the eleventh word, a zero stored to the settle word at +0x9750, then `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, `0x10658` and `0x120000`. It then stores the extent at range +0x18 and the mode at range +0x1c. When the word at group +0x50 is zero it walks the first two words of the block that the pointer at context +0x16bac names, stops at the first one that holds -1 and stores the group's first word (its id) there, and stores nothing if neither holds -1. It sets the halfword at +6 of the counter record that range +0x24 points to (the 16-byte counter record) to 1 and adds 1 to its halfword at +4, then calls `__glOdysseyMIPS_SYNC` (0xdba4b90, local GOT entry 3005, a `jr ra` whose delay slot is `sync`). The ioctl is skipped, returning 1, only when the counter record's halfword at +2 is zero and its halfword at +0 is nonzero (the two `bnezl` at 0xdaace34 and 0xdaace40); otherwise it builds a request at sp+48 (the group's first word, then the halfwords from range +8 and range +0xc) and sends ioctl `0x6f640003` through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0). A zero result returns 1. A nonzero result takes 1 off the +4 halfword, zeroes the +6 halfword, calls `__glOdysseyMIPS_SYNC` again and returns 0. Differences from the 6.5.30 N32 routine: the same drain words and ioctl, with this build's offsets (the block pointer at +0x16bac where N32 uses +0x16c9c, the settle word at +0x9750 where N32 uses +0x9830), and a `sync` after each counter change, which N32 does not do.
- `__glOdysseyGetHardwareShadow` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdabf724, 96 bytes):
  **Established.** Sends private ioctl `0x6f64000c` through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0, slot gp-30572) with the context (a0, kept at 32(sp)) and the second argument moved into a2 as the data pointer. A result of zero or more is returned unchanged. A negative result makes it call the function at offset 20 of the context, with the context and the string at 0xdbbb940 ("ODSY_CTXMGR_SAVE_HW_SHADOW: failed", read from the library's .rodata, local GOT entry at gp-32644 plus -18112), and return what that call returns. The shape matches the 6.5.30 o32 routine, with this build's own gp constants and string address.
- `__glOdysseyAssignToPMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaaca14, 368 bytes):
  **Established.** Sends host data to a pipe-memory object (arguments: a0 context, a1 group id, a2 entry index, a3 host address, and on the stack the length, the flags, a caller word and a pointer to the eight-word transfer header at 148(sp)). It first masks the header's word at +20 with 0x1ffff in place, then fills an 80-byte request at sp+40 that `bzero` (slot gp-17196) cleared: 1 is kept in the separate word at sp+36, the group id at request +4, the entry index (a2, stored with `sh` at sp+48), the host address at +0x14 with its sign extension in the word at +0x10, the length at +0x18, the eight header words at +0x1c to +0x38, the flags with bit 0 forced on at +0x3c and the caller word at +0x40. It calls `CheckMMXfrArgs` (0xdaac9ac) with the request and returns 0 when that returns 0. It sends ioctl `0x6f640006` (slot gp-30572); a nonzero result puts 0 in sp+36 and returns it. On success it stores zero at context +0x21de0 when bit 1 of the request's flags word is set (the caller asked for a wait), and otherwise the request word at +0x4c, which the kernel rewrote; it returns 1. This build's context offset is 0x21de0 where the 6.5.30 N32 routine uses +0x21ee8.
- `__glOdysseyReadFromPMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaacb84, 320 bytes):
  **Established.** The read direction of the same transfer, with the same arguments and request layout (header word +20 masked with 0x1ffff in place, the sign-extended host address at request +0x10 and +0x14, the same `CheckMMXfrArgs` check, ioctl `0x6f640006`, the same result handling, zero or the request word at +0x4c stored at context +0x21de0 and 1 returned). The only difference from `__glOdysseyAssignToPMObj` is that the flags are stored exactly as passed (the sixth argument, passed on the stack, goes to request +0x3c unchanged, with no `ori 1`). It keeps only a0 in a stack slot (120(sp)) because it never reads a1 to a3 again after storing them.
- `__glOdysseyCreatePMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaac57c, 464 bytes):
  **Established.** Asks the kernel for a new memory object (arguments: a0 context, a1 pointer to a ten-word description, a2 pointer to the object number, which is read and rewritten, a3 a halfword). The request at sp+56 holds -1 at +0, the number from a2 at +4, a3 as a halfword at +8, the ten words of the description at +0xc to +0x33 (copied three words per pass for three passes and then one word) and 0 at +0x34. It sends ioctl `0x6f640001` through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0). When the ioctl returns zero and the number the kernel left at request +4 is -1 it returns 0 and stores nothing. When it returns zero and that number is not -1 it stores the number through a2, and, when bit 1 of the request word at +0x34 is set, also stores the request word at +0x3c into the table at +0x1c of the kernel record named by the word at context +0x16ba4, at the index given by the halfword at request +0x40; it returns 1 either way. When the ioctl returns nonzero it returns 0: unless the request number is -1 and the caller's own number (the word a2 points at) is not -1, it does nothing more; in that one case it first walks every context in the sharing set and removes that number's group record from each (`InitCSetCursor` at 0xdaae0b4, then `NextCSetElem` at 0xdaae0c8 on the word at +0x16ba4, `LookupKnownGroup` with creation off, `RemoveKnownGroup` at 0xdaadbb0), then stores the -1 through a2. The store to a2 at 0xdaac700 is unreachable: it follows the unconditional branch at 0xdaac6f8 and nothing branches to it; the branch-likely `bnel` at 0xdaac6f0 stores the number in its own delay slot.
- `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObj` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaac868, 324 bytes):
  **Established.** Frees a memory object (arguments: a0 context, a1 pointer to the object number, a2 a count). It returns 0 at once when a2 is negative or 17 or more. It fills a request at sp+48 with the number at +4, a2 as a halfword at +8 and 1 at +0x34 (the +0 word is not set), and sends ioctl `0x6f640002` through `__glOdysseyIoctl`; a nonzero result returns 0. When the request's word at +0x34 is nonzero afterwards (the kernel keeps the object) it returns 1. Otherwise it walks every context in the sharing set (`InitCSetCursor` then `NextCSetElem` on the word at context +0x16ba4), calling `LookupKnownGroup` with creation off and the number and, where one is found, `RemoveKnownGroup`; it then stores -1 through a1 and returns 1.
- `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObjGroup` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaac74c, 284 bytes):
  **Established.** Frees a whole memory object, in the opposite order to `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObj` (arguments: a0 context, a1 pointer to the object number). It first walks every context in the sharing set and removes the number's group record from each (the same `InitCSetCursor`, `NextCSetElem`, `LookupKnownGroup` with creation off, `RemoveKnownGroup` loop on the word at +0x16ba4), then sends ioctl `0x6f640002` with the number at request +4 and 0 at +0x34 (the +0 and +8 words are not set). A nonzero result returns 0; a zero result stores -1 through a1 and returns 1.
- `LookupKnownGroup` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaad6e4, 356 bytes):
  **Established.** Finds the group record for an object number (arguments: a0 context, a1 number, a2 create flag at 64(sp)). The numbers -3 and -4 return 0. Otherwise `LookupCSetId` (0xdaae27c) is called on the set at context +0x16b88 and a nonzero answer is returned. With no record stored it returns 0 unless the create flag is set; with the flag it calls the context's allocator (the function at offset 0 of the context) for 84 bytes. A zero from the allocator raises `__glSetError` 1285 (0x505) and returns 0. Otherwise it zeroes the 84 bytes with `bzero`, stores the number in the first word, calls `AddKnownGroup` (0xdaadb54) on the set at +0x16b88 and ignores its result, and calls `LookupCoresidRange` (0xdaad848) with level 0, count 15 and create on. When that returns a record it returns the new group record; otherwise it calls `RemoveKnownGroup` (0xdaadbb0) on the group and returns 0. The first `beq` at 0xdaad708 skips the -4 test when the number is -3 and branches to the return of 0.
- `LookupCoresidRange` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaad848, 624 bytes):
  **Established.** Finds, or asks the kernel to describe, a range record of a group (arguments: a0 context, a1 group, a2 level, a3 count, the create flag at 112(sp)). It scans the list whose head is the word at group +0xc plus four times the level and returns the first record whose count at +0xc is not below a3 (the scan uses branch-likely `beqzl` and `bnezl`). With no match and the create flag zero it returns 0. With the flag it takes 44 bytes from the context's allocator (a zero raises `__glSetError` 1285 and returns 0), zeroes them and presets the record: group at +4, level at +8, count at +0xc, zeros at +0, +0x10 and +0x14, -1 at +0x18 and +0x1c. It sends ioctl `0x6f640004` with a request at sp+48: the word 2, the group's first word at sp+52, and the level and count as halfwords at sp+56 and sp+58. On a nonzero result it releases the record through the context's release entry (the function at offset 12) and returns 0. On a zero result the kernel has overwritten the request buffer, and the routine reads these reply fields (the routine does not store them; byte numbers count from the buffer start at sp+48): when bit 4 of the reply's first word is set it stores the reply word at byte 28 of the request buffer into the word at context +0x16bac plus four times the reply halfword at bytes 32 and 33; it stores the reply halfword at bytes 12 and 13 (the object handle) in group +4, sets group +8 to the table word at [kernel record at +0x16ba4] +0x1c plus four times the handle's high byte, plus 0x40 times its low byte, and stores the reply halfword at bytes 14 and 15 in group +0x50; it stores the reply halfword at bytes 16 and 17 in record +0x20 and points record +0x28 at the status halfword at [+0x16ba8] plus twice that index; it stores the reply halfword at bytes 18 and 19 in record +0x22 and points record +0x24 at the 16-byte counter record that the index's low ten bits select in the block named by its high six bits (blocks from +0x16bac); it copies the status halfword into the counter record's +4, zeroes its +6 and +8, links the record at the head of the level's list and returns it. This is the N32 routine's algorithm with this build's offsets (+0x16ba4, +0x16ba8 and +0x16bac where N32 uses +0x16c94, +0x16c98 and +0x16c9c).
- `__glOdysseyFindWindowSize` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda44c9c, 412 bytes):
  **Established.** Refreshes the context's window origin and size (argument: a0 context; no value is set on return). It seeds the width and height locals with the words at +0xe6c and +0xe70, then calls the callback at context +0x1c with the context, two origin locals, the width local and (fifth argument, on the stack) the height local. Unlike the 6.5.30 N32 routine it has no kernel query: there is no ioctl and no byte test before the callback. It stores the float of the returned origin x plus the float at +0x19cb4 at +0x19cac, and the float of origin y plus the same float at +0x19cb0. It compares the returned width and height with +0xe6c and +0xe70 and calls `__glChangeWindowSize` (0xdb1aeb8, local GOT entry 1457) with the context, width and height when either differs (the height compare is branch-likely). It then calls the callback at +0x48 with the context for the read-window width and height, compares them with +0xe74 and +0xe78, and calls `__glChangeReadWindowSize` (0xdb1afd0, entry 1458) with them when either differs. When the word at +0x168f0 is nonzero it calls `__glChangeWindowSize` again with the words at +0x6a0 and +0x6a4. Finally, when the byte at +0xc90 is nonzero and the draw width or height from the first callback still differs from +0xe6c or +0xe70 as they stand after those calls, it sets the byte at +0x11fc0 to 1. The 6.5.30 N32 routine makes the read-window callback conditional on a byte (+0xdd0 there) and kicks off with the kernel query described in its entry; this build does neither.
- `__glOdysseyDriverMakeCurrent` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdac006c, 880 bytes):
  **Established.** Asks the kernel to bind the context's drawable and records the reply (argument: a0 context; returns 1 on success, 0 after reporting a failure). The request buffer starts at sp+44: its first word is 2 when the word at context +0xcdc is positive, plus 4 when the byte at +0xc8c is zero; the word at +0x28 of the buffer (sp+84) is 2 when the word at +0xd7c is positive, plus 4 when the byte at +0xd2c is zero. It sends ioctl `0x6f640008` through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0). A negative result calls the function at context +0x14 with the context and the string "odsy: MakeCurrent ioctl failed" (0xdbbb990, local GOT entry at gp-32644 plus -18032) and returns 0. On success the kernel has overwritten the buffer, and the routine copies from it (byte numbers count from the buffer start at sp+44): the word at byte 4 to +0x16c50, the word at byte 8 to +0x16c54, byte 34 to +0x16c58, the halfword at byte 32 to +0x16c5c and byte 35 to +0x16c60. The byte at buffer +36 selects the next block. With 0 it zeroes 80 bytes from +0x16c78 with `bzero`. With 1 it stores the word at byte 12 to +0x16c78, the word at byte 8 to +0x16c7c, byte 37 to +0x16c80, the halfword at byte 32 to +0x16c84, the word at byte 16 to +0x16c8c, then byte 8 to +0x16c90, byte 37 to +0x16c94 and the halfword at byte 32 to +0x16c98, and zeroes 20 bytes at +0x16ca0 and at +0x16cb4. With 2 it stores the same eight words, then the word at byte 20 to +0x16ca0, byte 8 to +0x16ca4, byte 37 to +0x16ca8, the halfword to +0x16cac, the word at byte 24 to +0x16cb4, byte 8 to +0x16cb8, byte 37 to +0x16cbc and the halfword to +0x16cc0. Any other selector skips the whole block. Every successful path then stores the words at bytes 44 and 48 to +0x16c64 and +0x16c68, byte 74 to +0x16c6c, the halfword at byte 72 to +0x16c70 and byte 75 to +0x16c74, and returns 1. The N32 routine has the same stages at its own offsets (+0x16d40 onward).
- `__glOdysseySetProgram` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdab0010, 920 bytes):
  **Established.** Makes the transform program numbered by a1 the one the board runs (argument: a0 context; no value is set on return). The FIFO is the absolute 0x4000 window and `enc(v)` below is `(v & 0x3ff) | ((v >> 10) << 23)`. When the byte at +0x21dec is set it sends only `0x29808062` and returns. When the program count at +0xa574 is zero it returns having sent nothing (a branch-likely). Otherwise it: (1) takes the instruction address last patched (the word at +0xa5d0) and the loaded overlay start (+0xa5cc); the original record number is the patched address when the start is zero or above it, and otherwise that address minus the start plus 1848; it sends `0x27008000 | enc(patched address)` and six words `0x40008000 | enc(h)` from the six halfwords of the 12-byte record at that number in the table named by +0x16c44, from the sixth to the first. (2) Stores a1 at +0xa5c8 and takes the 32-byte record at +0xa578 plus 32 times a1. When that record's word +0x14 is nonzero and differs from the loaded start, it loads min(the count at +0x16c48 minus that word, 199) image records from that index to write addresses 1848, 1849 and so on, each as an address word and six halfword words, and stores the new start at +0xa5cc. (3) Stores the record's word +0x18 at +0xa5d0, and sends `0x27008000 | enc(that address)` and the six halfwords of the image record that the record's word +0x1c names. (4) Stores the record's word +0x10 plus one at +0xa140 and the list entry at +0xa174 plus 4 times word +0x10 at +0xa118; sends `0x29008000 | enc(entry)`, `0x33008000 | enc(+0xa140)`, `0x90808800`, 0 and 0. (5) Stores the record's words +0xc, +0x0, +0x4 and +0x8 at +0xa140, +0xa118, +0xa11c and +0xa124 and sends them as `0x33008000`, `0x29008000`, `0x2a008000` and `0x2c008000` with the matching `enc`. This is the N32 routine's algorithm with this build's offsets and a different early-out word (`0x29808062` where N32 sends `0x298080c5`).
- `__glOdysseyInitLighting` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdaa6730, 864 bytes):

It sets the two words at +0x16958 and +0x1695c to 255 (the second is stored and then read back to fill the first). It then sends, to the absolute window 0x4000, ten attribute words and their operands, in this order: `0xc18093ff` with 0, 0, 1.0, 1.0; `0xc180cc19` with 0, 0, 1.0 (three operands); `0xc680d02f` with 0.2, 0.2, 0.2, 1.0; `0xc780d030` with 0.8, 0.8, 0.8, 1.0; `0xc880d031` with 0, 0, 0, 1.0; `0xc980d032` with 0, 0, 0, 1.0; `0xca80d035`, `0xcb80d036`, `0xcc80d037` and `0xcd80d038` with the same four operand sets as the four before them. - `__glOdysseySetDrawbuf` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xdac03dc, 864 bytes):
  **Established.** Selects the buffers drawn to and resynchronises the board (arguments: a0 context, a1, a2, a3, and the fourth flag as the byte at 75(sp), the low byte of the stack word). The mask at sp+52 is the sum of 1 when the low byte of a1 is nonzero, 2 for a2, 4 for a3 and 8 for the stack byte (the store at 0xdac044c carries bit 8 only when that byte is nonzero; the `beqzl` at 0xdac042c loads the byte on both paths). It sends ioctl `0x6f640009` with that word through `__glOdysseyIoctl` (0xdabf6b0, slot gp-30572). A nonzero result calls the function at context +0x14 with the context and the string " can't set drawbuf" at 0xdbbb9b0 (the slot at gp-32644 plus -18000) and carries on. It then sends to 0x4000: the 15-word settle sequence (`0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, `0x10019`, `0x10443`, 150, four `0x10046`, a zero stored to +0x9750 after the eleventh word, `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`). Then, when bit 27 of the word at +0x95c4 is set, it sends `0x10841`, 0, 0, `0x1204b`, 0, `0x00ffffff`, five zeros, 0, `0x10840`, 0, 0 (a block that the code builds from constants); when clear it sends the single word `0x90008116`. It then sends `0x11017` and the four words at +0x95bc, +0x95c0, +0x95c4 and +0x95c8; a short settle (`0x10443`, 150, four `0x10046`), the clear of +0x9750 and `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`; `0x11017` with the words at +0x95bc and +0x95c0, a zero in the third place and the word at +0x95c8; `0x11017` with the same words but the word at +0x95c4 with bit 23 (0x800000) set in the third place; the full settle sequence again (with `0x10019`, 150 and the clear); the same bit-27 choice between the long block and `0x90008116` (the `beqzl` at 0xdac0684 sends `0x90008116` in its delay slot); `0x11017` with the four words at +0x95bc to +0x95c8; a short settle and the clear of +0x9750; and `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046` at the return (the last `0x10046` in the delay slot). The store at 0xdac06d4 is unreachable. The return value is whatever v0 holds (`0x10046`). The 6.5.30 N32 entry describes the same bracket structure.
- `ApplyViewport` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda44e38, 536 bytes):
  **Established.** Recomputes the viewport-dependent state after a window change (argument: a0 context; v0 holds a0 plus 0x8000 on return, which is incidental). It saves the word at +0xe70 (the old window height) at sp+36, calls `__glOdysseyFindWindowSize` (0xda44c9c, local GOT entry 137) and `__glUpdateViewport` (0xdb84834, entry 2217), each with the context. It then reads the window width +0xe6c and height +0xe70 (kept at sp+60 and sp+56) and the viewport words +0x698, +0x69c, +0x6a0 and +0x6a4 (x, y, width, height by their use); sp+52 and sp+48 keep +0x698 and +0x69c, sp+44 keeps +0x6a0 plus +0x698 and sp+40 keeps +0x6a4 plus +0x69c. It stores the float minus (float(+0x6a4) times the float at +0xdcc) at +0x6c0, and at +0x6c4 the float (float(+0xe70) minus the float at +0xe54) minus (float(+0x69c) plus float(+0x6a4) times the float at +0xdcc) plus the float at +0xe50. It adds the float of (new height minus the saved old height) to the float at +0x174. It calls `__glOdysseyImageDescribeWindow` (0xda6b350, entry 289) with the address context +0x11c98, 0, and the word +0x698 in a2 (a3 is whatever the register holds; nothing sets it), and again with context +0x11cd0 and 1; then it calls the function at context +0x2de4 with the context. It sets the byte at +0x73e4 to 1 when +0x698 and +0x69c are both non-negative, the window width is not below +0x6a0 plus +0x698 and the window height is not below +0x6a4 plus +0x69c (all four signed compares fall through), and to 0 otherwise. When the byte's value changed it stores 2 in process word 0x1018 and sets bit 0 of the word at +0x2d44. It replaces the low 12 bits of the word at +0x9748 with the low 12 bits of +0xe70. When the settle word at +0x9750 is set it clears it and sends `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046` to 0x4000; it always then sends `0x10456` and the word at +0x9748. The 6.5.30 N32 routine has this plan with its own offsets and also describes two more windows when two bytes (+0xc98 and +0x120f5 there) are set; this build has no such calls.
- `__glOdysseyClear` (`builds/vpro-patch-4103/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY/libGLcore.so` 0xda44070, 2068 bytes):
  **Established.** Clears a window rectangle by turning the pipeline into a flat fill and drawing one rectangle (arguments: a0 context, a1 pointer to four colour floats, a2 the depth as the bits of a float, a3 the write mask whose low byte is used, then on the stack the rectangle's x, y, width and height at 160(sp), 164(sp), 168(sp) and 172(sp) and a flag whose low byte is at 179(sp)). The 6.5.30 N32 routine takes a double depth and can fill through the kernel (ioctl `0x6f64000e`); this routine never calls `__glOdysseyIoctl`. It saves the words at +0x7b1c, +0x7b20, +0x95c4, +0x7b50 and +0x7b4c at sp+140, sp+136, sp+128, sp+68 and sp+64, and rewrites them. In +0x7b1c it clears bits 14, 17, 8, 22, 19, 20, 2, 4, 7 and 11, clears and sets bit 12, then clears and sets bits 16 and 15 later; the end result is bits 12, 15 and 16 set and the others cleared. In +0x7b20 it clears bits 5:0 and 11:10 and sets bits 15:13 to 1. In +0x7b50 it sets bits 2:0. In +0x7b4c it sets bits 27:25 to 7, puts the low byte of a3 in bits 7:0, sets bits 15:8 to 0xff, and sets bits 24:22, 21:19 and 18:16 each to 2. It raises bit 1 and then bit 2 of the dirty word at +0x168d0, each time storing 2 in process word 0x1018 and rewriting +0x2d44 with itself. It keeps bit 24 and bits 19:12 of the word at +0x7b7c at sp+92 and sp+88. It sends, each after the settle prefix (`0x10443`, 250, `0x10046`, `0x10046`, with a zero stored to +0x9750 first) when +0x9750 is set, `0x10420` with +0x7b50 and `0x1041f` with +0x7b4c.
  It then computes a flag a1 from the words +0x7b7c, +0x95c4 and +0x7a90 and the stack flag byte. Branch on bit 19 of +0x95c4. When it is clear a1 is 1 if bits 20, 21, 22 and 23 of +0x7b7c are all equal. When it is set a1 is 1 if the low 12 bits of +0x7b7c are 0 or 0xfff, and it is then forced to 0 unless bit 5 of +0x7a90 is set or (bits 6:4 of +0x95c4 are 0 and either bits 12:10 are 0 with bits 9:7 equal to 1, or bits 12:10 are 2 with bits 9:7 equal to 4). In both cases a1 is then ANDed with: (bits 19:12 of +0x7b7c are 0xff and bit 24 is set) or (bits 19:12 are 0 and bit 24 is clear), otherwise 0; then, when bits 12:10 of +0x95c4 are 0, with whether the stack flag byte is nonzero; then it is forced to 0 when bit 4 of +0x7a90 is clear. Bit 23 of +0x95c4 receives a1, and bit 31 receives a1 AND the stack flag byte's truth when bit 6 of +0x7a90 is set (zero otherwise). It then sets bit 21 of the dirty word, stores 2 in process word 0x1018, rewrites +0x2d44, and sets the low nibble of +0x7b24 to 1010 (bits 3:2 to 10, bits 1:0 to 10) and sends `0x10415` with it. When the dirty word at +0x168d0 is nonzero it calls `__glOdysseyValidateHardware` (0xda47694, slot gp-32192).
  The fill: when a1 is nonzero it sends `0x14400`, 10, `0x8f80a92f`; when zero it sends `0x14400`, 7, `0x8f80a944`. Both then send the four colour floats, the corner (float(x), float(y), z) and the corner (float(x plus width), float(y plus height), z) with z the depth argument, store 1 in the settle word at +0x9750 and send `0x14001` as the last word. It then restores +0x95c4, +0x7b24 (the value it held on entry), +0x7b50, +0x7b4c, +0x7b1c and +0x7b20 from the saved copies, sets bits 1, 2 and 21 of the dirty word, stores 2 in process word 0x1018, rewrites +0x2d44, and sends `0x10415` with +0x7b24, `0x10420` with +0x7b50 and `0x1041f` with +0x7b4c, each after a settle prefix when +0x9750 is set (all three tests are branch-likely, and the block word is stored in the delay slot when the prefix is skipped). It sets no return value.
